# Deferred obligations ledger

> **Maintained companion — NOT a generated ticket.** Committed and hand-maintained; preserved
> across any ticket regeneration; never overwritten or deleted. It has no `NN_` sequence prefix
> so it is not mistaken for a chain ticket.

The rules:
1. **Every run, first:** read this file. If the ticket you are about to implement — or a
   prerequisite it depends on — unblocks any `OPEN` row, **closing that row is part of your
   run**: verify it for real, then flip it to `DONE` with the date and evidence.
2. **Never delete a row.** Flip `OPEN` → `DONE` (verified) or `WONTFIX` (with a reason).
   History stays.
3. **When you defer something new,** append a row here in the same run that defers it. A
   deferral that is not in this file did not happen.
4. **Gates refuse to pass** while any `OPEN` row scoped to that phase remains. Treat an open
   row as gate-blocking.

Status values: `OPEN` (owed) · `PARTIAL` · `DONE` (verified — add date + evidence) ·
`WONTFIX` (add reason) · `ACCEPTED-SKELETON`. `kind`: V (verification) | F (functionality) |
D (deviation) | H (handoff seam) | P (human prerequisite) | X (other).

Pre-existing normalized debt is tracked in `docs/build/BACKLOG.csv`; each row below cites its
`BL-` id where one exists (no duplication). These rows are the **RETURN PASS** obligations from
`docs/build/LEDGER.md` — Phase-21 tickets the operator ran with a human gate skipped. They are
**not** blocks (the chain completed and the round-1 capstone is DONE); they are owed operator
work re-run per each ticket's `Run:` line after the operator acts.

## P26.18 (SOURCES.17) — hosted land of the now-chunked OSM source (`camreg_osm_surveillance`)

The chunked-commit write path (`PgClaimSink.assert_claims` commits in bounded
`commit_chunk_size` transactions) is landed and proven deterministically on a
real PG18+PostGIS testcontainer: full re-run = +0, an interrupted-after-k-chunks
run resumes to the exact uninterrupted count, and no UPDATE/DELETE is issued at
runtime (`tests/db/test_claim_sink.py`, 7/7 green under `SIG_REQUIRE_DB_TESTS=1`).
The remaining leg is the **hosted land itself**: chunking makes the ~1.37M-claim
OSM ingest *durable/resumable* (surviving the Cloud Run task deadline) but not
*faster* — at the measured `db-f1-micro` throughput (~20–45 committed claims/s,
P26.16) the land is a **~12–13h operation**, and proving the idempotent hosted
re-run (+0) requires that first land to COMPLETE first. Neither fits a bounded
autonomous session, so the hosted green is recorded here honestly rather than
fabricated (implement-spec 5.3 / BM-DEFER-02). GCP access exists in this
environment (`14stevevitali@gmail.com`, project `zeta-medley-508121-u7`), so this
is a *time/scale* deferral, not a credential one.

## P27.5 (LAUNCH.5) — presentation analytics not emitted by the export (2026-09-22)

P27.5 routed every public surface through the export data seam (`web/src/lib/data.ts`): the nine
frozen P27.1 surface artifacts are read fixtures|export and FAIL LOUD in export mode on a missing
artifact. Three classes of page data are NOT part of the frozen P27.1 export contract and so are
served through the seam as committed constants in BOTH modes (never a fail-loud artifact): the map's
national-view density bins, the network explorer's centrality/ER-quality analytics + focus entity,
and the page-scoped presentation constants (decision point, capture diff, provenance summaries,
jurisdiction claims, editorial review/rationale templates, reference-page exemplars). Also: the
DB-less fixture `sig-exports build --jurisdiction okc` path emits only `dossiers.json`/`leverage.json`
(not the ten surface JSONs), so export-mode exercise uses the pure-web `npm run export:fixtures`
overlay harness (or a real `--from-spine` bundle, D-P27.4-1).

## P30.1 (GO-LIVE.1) — OSM-land write-path throughput follow-ups (2026-09-24)

The hosted OSM land (D-SOURCES.17-1) completed and was verified, but it exposed two write-path
costs. A restart of `sig-ops scheduled-ingest` is an idempotent **full replay** (re-fetch all 158
ArcGIS pages ≈57 MB, then walk all ~1.37M records with content-keyed `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` —
`connectors/src/connectors/pipeline.py` fetch loop + single `assert_claims`), not a checkpoint; and
the replay was **latency-bound** (one round-trip per claim/prerequisite insert in
`PgClaimSink._insert_claim`), not DB-bound. Neither is a correctness defect (append-only + idempotency
held); both are recorded as follow-ups here, deliberately NOT implemented in P30.1 (a
verification/measurement ticket), and home to **BL-057**.

| id | kind | item | why deferred | unblocked by | how to verify | proxy now | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-P30.1-1 | F | batch / pipeline-mode writes in `PgClaimSink` — cut per-claim round-trips (psycopg pipeline mode and/or multi-row `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING RETURNING` per chunk, with the `claim_evidence` links written in the same chunk transaction) so a large land or replay is DB-bound rather than latency-bound | out of P30.1 scope (a hosted verification ticket — no write-path change); the land already completed and verified correctly; changing the sink needs its own ticket + the `tests/db/test_claim_sink.py` idempotency/resumability proofs re-run | a scheduled write-path ticket (BL-057) | `SIG_REQUIRE_DB_TESTS=1 uv run pytest tests/db/test_claim_sink.py` stays green (append-only, +0 replay, interrupted-resume = uninterrupted count, no UPDATE/DELETE) and a measured claims/min on a seeded ≥100k-claim land shows the round-trip reduction vs the row-at-a-time baseline (P30.1 hosted: ~400–470 inserts/min on `db-f1-micro`; on `db-custom-2-8192` the tjqdq run took 5h10m for a full 158-page fetch + 1,369,210-record walk that inserted 284,682 new claims) | row-at-a-time chunked path: correct, idempotent, resumable — just slow | DONE 2026-09-24 (P31.3 / HARDEN.3, ADR-110; cites BL-057) — opened OPEN 2026-09-24 (cites BL-057) · **Round 9 seed 2026-09-24:** scheduled → **P31.3** (manifest row 144, Wave A). Stays OPEN until P31.3 verifies it. · **P31.3 (2026-09-24): DONE.** `PgClaimSink` stages claims per chunk and writes each chunk with multi-row `INSERT … SELECT FROM unnest … ON CONFLICT (content_digest) DO NOTHING RETURNING`, plus one `claim_evidence` INSERT per 2,000-row batch, inside the chunk transaction. all pre-existing behaviour surfaced by that smoke (none is a regression of the roll). Homes to **BL-057**.

the produced `CANDIDATE_MANIFEST.json` validates (frame check `consistent`, `materialization.plus_zero`, validation `complete`, `pointer_unchanged: true`, `published: false`); the hosted `r/<pub>` namespace exists unpublished and `latest.json` is byte-identical before/after | the obligation is committed and machine-checkable — `docs/build/reports/p32.23a-release-candidate/LIVE_RETURN_PASS.json` (`candidate-return-pass/1`, `prepared_not_executed`): the hosted freeze + build commands, the verification checklist, and the explicit reservations (no publication, no gate completion, no pointer write, no evaluation claim) | OPEN 2026-10-19 (P32.23a; cites BL-058) · **P32.24 (2026-10-20) — stays OPEN:** the acceptance run verified this candidate's packet read-only and recorded `candidate.record_surface` + `B.candidate_network` as `not_applicable` — the fixture-seeded spine publishes 0 records and an empty network, so record/relationship journey semantics could NOT be verified on this artifact and ran on the declared acceptance corpus instead; they must re-run against the production candidate the `D-R10-LIVE-1` return pass produces. · **P32.25 (2026-10-20, ADR-144) — stays OPEN:** the publish + verify + rollback rehearsal executed over the *fixture* artifact set proves the machinery, not the production candidate — the hosted repaired-snapshot build this row owes is unchanged: `sig-ops release-candidate` over the hosted spine, its own validation + return pass. Row stays OPEN. · **P33.3 capstone closure (2026-09-28) — stays OPEN:** adjudicated and carried into the Round-10 acceptance register (`docs/build/CAPSTONE_CLOSURE.md` §(f5)) with exact owner, landing and compensating control; presented to the Round-10 acceptance gate (`ACCEPT-R10`, manifest row 195) — not closed. · re-homed 2026-10-01 (SEED-15, Round-11 seed T4; append-only): (cites BL-085) — every BL row this row cited before is closed or accepted; the live home in SEED-14b's re-homing map (docs/build/runs/SEED-14b.md) |
